Alternative-fuel share of ship orders falls amid low-carbon challenges: Wärtsilä
Alternative-fuel ship orders drop to 17%
Limited green fuel supplies prolong industry transition
Overall orders surge on strong shipping markets
Shipping companies have shown less appetite for alternative marine fuels in newbuild investments this year despite ordering more vessels, marine equipment maker Wärtsilä said Oct. 27, highlighting the industry's challenges in transitioning to lowcarbon energy.
A total of 446 alternative-fuel-capable ships were ordered in January-September, representing 17% of all contracted vessels, down from 22% a year earlier, Wärtsilä said in its third-quarter report, citing Clarksons Research.
By capacity, their share fell more sharply to 30% from 48%. "Limited availability, infrastructure constraints, higher costs, and a lack of global emission regulations continue to slow the large-scale deployment of sustainable fuels," the Finnish company said. "This has resulted in a more gradual and uneven transition, but one that is increasingly influencing commercial decisions, investment priorities, and long-term strategy throughout the maritime industry." September's average bunker price for very low sulfur fuel oil was $16.92 per gigajoule in Rotterdam, compared with $26.15/GJ for LNG, $28.09 for B30 bioblends, and $50.03/GJ for bio-LNG, according to the Platts bunker cost calculator. Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. The International Maritime Organization had previously planned to implement the Net-Zero Framework from 2028 to impose a cost on maritime greenhouse gas emissions, but the regulatory timeline has been delayed amid strong opposition by the US and Middle Eastern oil producers. Overall, newbuilding contracting more than doubled to 2,552 ships in January-September from 1,200 over the same period,

according to the Wärtsilä report. "Supported by positive market sentiment and an aging
global fleet, newbuild investment has been strong in 2026," the company said. "Ordering has been mainly driven by demand for tankers and containerships, while activity has also remained healthy for cruise vessels and LNG carriers."
The sustained ordering boom has pushed shipyard orderbooks and lead times to their highest since 2009, with vessels on order equivalent to 23% of the existing global fleet's capacity, according to Wärtsilä.
Despite larger yard orderbooks, rising shipbuilding capacity and delivery volumes have kept average newbuild prices broadly stable, with average prices up just 1% year over year despite significant differences between vessel segments, the company added.
The global shipyard order book reached 203.2 million compensated gross tons as of Sept. 1, up 13% from a year earlier and just slightly below the all-time high of 210.4 million compensated gross tons in May, according to BIMCO.
Financial performance Wärtsilä's wider business continued to benefit from strong
equipment demand in the third quarter, with total order intake rising 33% year over year to 2.37 billion ($2.64 billion) and equipment orders jumping 53% to 1.42 billion.
Net sales increased 7% to 1.49 billion, while comparable operating profit rose 19% to 212 million, equivalent to 14.2% of sales compared with 12.8% a year earlier.
For January-September, order intake increased 26% to 7.30 billion, while the order book stood at 9.88 billion, 22% higher year over year. Wärtsilä expects demand for its marine equipment in Q4 2026-Q3 2027 to be similar to that of the year prior.
-- Max Lin
ECSA urge Brussels to triple EUA offers for low-carbon bunker shift
ECSA seeks 330 million allowances for SMAP in 2028-40
Shipowners say their contributions exceed low-carbon support
EU leaders aim to finalize carbon market reforms next quarter
Europe's largest shipowner association has reiterated its call on EU policymakers to triple emissions allowances earmarked to support a low-carbon bunker transition, arguing that the European Commission's proposal is too small to provide the investment certainty needed to scale up shipping decarbonization.
In an Oct. 27 statement, European Shipowners, also known as ECSA, said the proposed Sustainable Maritime Alternative Propulsion mechanism should be increased to offer at least 330 million EU Allowances from the European Commission's proposed 110 million over 2028-40.
In July, the EC proposed SMAP as part of a wider carbon

© 2026 by S&P Global Inc. All rights reserved.

23

©2026 S&P Global Inc. Any unauthorized use of this copyrighted material constitutes infringement of our intellectual property and will subject you to possible legal action.

Platts Oilgram Price Report

October 27, 2026

market reform. EU lawmakers and member states aim to agree on the exact incoming rules next quarter.
"Shipping pays almost 20% of total Emissions Trading System revenues, although it accounts for only 10% of the emissions covered by the system, and, unlike other sectors, it has never received a single free allowance," ECSA Secretary General Sotiris Raptis said in a statement.
Shipping companies are responsible for about 10% of greenhouse gas emissions covered by the EU ETS while purchasing approximately 90 million EUAs per year, equivalent to almost 20% of all allowances auctioned under the system, according to ECSA.
"The European Parliament and the Council should at least triple the ETS revenues earmarked for SMAP to send a credible market signal for the scale-up of sustainable maritime fuels and technologies, including energy efficiency," Raptis said.
Subsidy scheme Under the current proposal, SMAP would recycle ETS
allowances to partially offset the cost differential between conventional and lower-carbon marine energy.
EUAs would cover 55% of the price differential for biogas and advanced biofuels, 80% for low-carbon hydrogen and low-carbon fuels, and 90% for renewable fuels of non-biological origin. Eligible zero-emission propulsion technologies would receive 90% support.
But if claims from shipping companies exceed the available allowance reserve, the support provided for each claim would be reduced proportionately, according to the EC proposal.
September's average bunker costs for very low sulfur fuel oil in intra-EU trades were $1,005.74/metric ton in Rotterdam, compared with $1,378.55/mtVLSFOe for B30 bioblends and $2,057.17/mtVLSFOe for bio-LNG, according to the Platts bunker cost calculator when the ETS compliance costs were taken into account. Green ammonia cargo for delivery into Northwest Europe was $1,570.80/mtVLSFOe.
Assuming an EUA price of 100/metric ton of CO2 equivalent, ECSA said the shipping industry needs a total of 330 million allowances in 2028-2040 to cover the price gap between conventional, oil-based fuels and sustainable energy.
The nearest-December EUA contract has averaged 79.78/ mtCO2e so far this year, according to Platts assessments. Platts is part of S&P Global Energy.
ECSA said its modeling showed that the Commission's 110 million-EUA reserve would cover only one-third of what it estimates is needed to create a market for sustainable maritime fuels and technologies, leaving a shortfall of about 220 million allowances.
Sustainable fuels could be on average four times more expensive than conventional fuels, making predictable support important for investment decisions, according to ECSA. Its model assumes annual shipping energy demand of 27.5 million mt of oil equivalent within the relevant EU ETS.
"Predictability is essential: companies need certainty, before

they take investment decisions, that the price gap between sustainable and conventional fuels will be partly covered," Raptis said.
ECSA also called for SMAP eligibility to extend to all technologies that deliver carbon savings, including energyefficiency projects, and said companies should be able to seek support in advance rather than only after sustainable fuels or technologies are used.
Separately, the association backed proposed changes to ETS rules for offshore vessels but opposed having different compliance systems for 2027-30 and from 2031, arguing a single regime would reduce administrative burdens.
-- Max Lin

Platts European Fuel Oil Daily Market Analysis
Med HSFO faces tight supply from imports
High freight costs limit arbitrage flows
Power demand supports LSFO
The Mediterranean high sulfur fuel oil market was characterized by limited availability Oct. 27, as persistently elevated dirty freight rates continued to weigh on arbitrage opportunities.
There were resupply concerns in the HSFO market due to reduced imports from the Americas, participants noted.
"Nobody is bringing oil to the Med from the Caribbean now," one Mediterranean-based trader said. "Premiums are quite high now."
Reduced East Mediterranean production and blending challenges had further contributed to the firmer prices, a second source said.
On the demand side, there was "not much moving ... we see some bits and pieces of demand," a third trader said.
Softer bunker demand across the region, however, was balancing the supply fundamentals, contributing to a more balanced market.
In the European very low sulfur fuel oil market, premiums for product in Asia continued to support some movement of product out of Europe.
However, persistent freight costs had limited the volume of product that could move, therefore limiting support for European prices.
In the low sulfur fuel oil market, higher natural gas and LNG prices had supported 1% fuel oil power generation demand, despite the seasonally quieter period in the Mediterranean.
"There is demand from utilities in the Med which are pulling product from Northwest Europe to the Med," said a European trader.
This market, however, remained thinly traded, with a second market participant noting a lack of demand.
Trading activity in the Platts Market on Close assessment process was quiet Oct. 27, with no trades observed in the

Rotterdam VLSFO barge MOC and 4,000 mt for the HSFO equivalent. There were no trades for fuel oil cargoes.

Platts Asia and Middle Eastern Naphtha Daily Market Analysis
Olefin margins squeezed on high feedstock cost
South Korea's YNCC to operate No. 1, 2 Yeosu crackers at 70% in Nov
Kuwait Petroleum offers 35,000 mt of naphtha for Nov. 16-30 delivery
Firm Asian naphtha prices were weighed down by steam cracker economics on Oct. 27, with some operators considering lower run rates in November, market sources said.
The CFR Northeast Asia ethylene price spread to C+F Japan naphtha narrowed to $143.75/metric ton Oct. 26, down $15.75/ mt on the week. This was below the typical breakeven spread of $250/mt for integrated producers and $300-$350/mt for nonintegrated producers
"I think everyone will try to reduce runs, the economics are bad", a trade source said.
Reflecting the weaker economics, South Korea's YNCC plans to operate its No. 1 and No. 2 steam crackers in Yeosu at 70% in November, down from October at about 74%. The No. 1 cracker has the capacity to of 990,000 mt/year of ethylene, 450,000 mt/y of propylene, while the No.2 cracker is able to produce 915,000 mt/y of ethylene, 458,000 mt/y of propylene.
In the derivatives market, brokers pegged the front-month November-December Mean of Platts Japan swaps time spread at $42/mt in midafternoon Asian trading Oct. 27, down $4.50/mt from the previous close.
In the spot market, Kuwait Petroleum has issued a tender offering 35,000 mt of full range naphtha for Nov. 16-30 delivery to Chiba on a DES basis via Salsa. The tender will close on Oct. 29, with same-day validity. Previously, the company has awarded its tender for 85,000 mt of full range naphtha, with a minimum 70% paraffin content for Nov 1-11 delivery to Chiba on a DES basis via the Torm Hedwig, closed Oct 13.
In statistical news, South Korea's naphtha stocks edged 3.1% higher month over month and 3.4% year over year to 14.826 million barrels in September, attributed to higher import volumes and a decline in exports, data from state-run Korea National Oil Corp. showed Oct. 26.
Naphtha imports climbed 24.50% from August but fell 47.80% from a year earlier to 11.452 million barrels in September.
Domestic naphtha production was down 3.7% month over month and increased 9.7% year over year at 24.322 million barrels.

Platts European Naphtha Daily Market Analysis
Asian demand tightens Atlantic supply
Gasoline weakness helps ease fundamentals
The European naphtha market fell Oct. 27 amid lower backwardation across the board and softer outright prices.
Strong demand in Asia and high east-west spreads have incentivized the pull of product from the Atlantic Basin into Asian demand outlets, tightening the picture in Europe and the Mediterranean.
"I think [the] European picture is more constructive, barrels [are] moving east," a trading source said.
Gasoline has softened significantly over the past few weeks, eroding the gasoline-naphtha spread and weighing down naphtha blending margins.
The easing margins have reduced blending demand, allowing more barrels previously absorbed by the gasoline pool to move into the petrochemical sector, which players expect will increasingly gain importance as a price setter.
"What's helping a bit is gasoline margins cooling off, so [I'm] hearing more blending barrels ending up in the petchem pull," the trading source said.
Another player active in the European naphtha market said: "[There are] much weaker premiums on blending, [and] still very healthy premiums on cracking."
However, steep backwardation continued to curb buying appetite, with consumers largely limiting purchases to minimum requirements and employing a hand-to-mouth approach.
"[It's] just too high [a] backwardation, people [are] trying to buy the bare minimum they need," the trading source added.
Platts assessed the physical naphtha CIF NWE cargo price at $957.75/mt, down $24.25/mt day over day.
Platts assessed the front-month CIF NWE naphtha swap down $22.75/mt at $931/mt, while the crack was up 5 cents/b at minus $4.75/b.
Platts assessed the November/December time spread at $41.75/mt, down $2.25/mt on the day, while the December/ January spread fell $2.75/mt to $25.75/mt.
The front-month East-West spread -- the spread between the

CFR Japan naphtha cargo swap and the CIF NWE equivalent -- was at $72/mt, down $4/mt on the day.

Platts European Propane Daily Commentary
Propane prices fall amid mixed demand signals
Winter restocking boosts coaster requirements
Naphtha discount narrows, weakening cracker appeal
European propane entered the late-October period Oct. 27, with seasonal replenishment requirements countered by weaker petrochemical buying incentives and competition for import supply.
Winter stock building had supported inland requirements,

increasing the importance of smaller parcels and the movement of imported product into downstream distribution networks.
However, restocking demand did not necessarily indicate a sustained increase in consumption, with the strength of heatingrelated offtake dependent on temperatures and the pace of inventory drawdowns.
Market conversations highlighted inland replenishment as a source of support for coaster demand.
"Europe is explosive," a trader said. However, petrochemical demand provided a more mixed influence. Propane's reduced discount to naphtha had weakened its relative attraction to flexible crackers, limiting the incentive to increase intake. Further buying would depend on feedstock economics and downstream production requirements, with a narrower discount potentially encouraging operators to favor alternative feeds. Internationally, planned Asian LPG cracking remained below September's actual consumption heading into November, reflecting challenging economics. The implication for Europe was a potentially less aggressive Asian petrochemical pull, although additional westbound supply would depend on freight-adjusted returns. A reduction in Eastern buying would only improve European availability if US-exported material became more attractive to European destinations. The balance, therefore, hinged on the interaction between winter consumption, cracker switching and import arrivals. Stronger heating offtake could absorb incoming volumes, while softer consumption would leave the market more dependent on petrochemical demand to clear supply. Platts European Butane Daily Commentary
Tight imports limit European supply access
Prices fall amid quiet trading conditions
European butane's fundamental backdrop on Oct. 27 centered on seasonal gasoline blending requirements, constrained import availability and reduced competitiveness within the petrochemical feedstock pool.
The transition to winter-grade gasoline strengthened the blending role of butane, providing an outlet as its relative value against naphtha reduced its appeal to flexible crackers.
Earlier discussions had identified this shift as an important influence on the European balance, thereby increasing the significance of gasoline-sector demand.
Seasonal specifications allowed greater butane incorporation into gasoline, although actual consumption would depend on blend economics, refinery operations and finished-product requirements.
However, the last two trading days have been described as quiet, with "blending interest calming," according to a trader.
Refinery retention for internal blending could also reduce merchant availability without requiring a corresponding decline in gross butane production.
Import supply developments added another consideration. Stronger US winter gasoline-blending demand reduced export availability to Asia, while fewer Middle Eastern sales restricted alternative supplies. Some Asian buyers encountered difficulties securing November-delivery cargoes, highlighting competition for available material. For Europe, these developments suggested that replacement supply could remain sensitive to competing destination returns. An attractive import arbitrage would require sufficient export availability, alongside workable freight and terminal access. Petrochemical consumption offered a potential balancing mechanism. The resulting European balance depended on whether seasonal blending absorbed volumes released by petrochemical buyers. Any easing in gasoline requirements could increase spot availability, while sustained refinery retention would limit the supply reaching buyers. In the butane CIF NWE large cargo market, no bids, offers, or trades were heard in the Platts Market on Close assessment process. In the absence of competitive indications, the market was assessed on trader sentiment. Platts East of Suez Dirty Tanker Daily Commentary
VLCC rises as fresh cargoes tighten tonnage
Suezmax edges higher alongside VLCC rebound
Southeast Asia Aframax falls; downside remains limited
The East of Suez VLCC market climbed higher Oct. 27 as fresh cargoes emerged, with more requirements heard to have been quietly quoted and worked in the market.
With more cargoes coming into the market and ships being taken, owners were in a stronger position to push for higher levels, a VLCC broker said.
"Owners will surely ask for more, but the market is still kind of quiet, so it shouldn't go too high," a VLCC charterer said.
Fixtures reported on Oct. 27 included New Energy, which was heard placed on subjects by Admic for a Nov. 5 loading from Fujairah to the Far East at w585. PTT was heard to have placed Kallista on subjects for a Nov. 11 Fujairah-Thailand voyage at w605.
Meanwhile, Glasford's Nov. 8 Fujairah-China cargo was heard covered, according to market participants, although further details were unavailable.
"The situation that created this market has not changed," another VLCC broker said.
The Suezmax market was assessed slightly higher, in line with market indications and supported by the rebound in the VLCC market.
The Sonangol Kalandula was placed on subjects by Mercuria for a 130,000 mt crude voyage from Fujairah to the East at an undisclosed rate and laycan.
Market sources said the vessel was heading east for drydocking.

The Aframax market moved lower out of Southeast Asia following fresh testing of the market.
The Hafnia Loire and Crescent River were reported taken by Vitol for Nov. 5-6 loading from Southeast Asia to Geelong at w690.
Market sources said there could be some further downside, although room for a significant correction appeared limited as the Southeast Asia tonnage list was becoming more balanced.
However, another Aframax broker saw potential for further weakness if activity remained subdued, while pointing to the Vancouver market as a key factor to watch.
"We have to watch whether the Vancouver market picks up, as a lot of the tonnage came from the West. If the market here slips further, a good percentage of those ships may have little interest in staying in the East," the broker said.
The MR market edged slightly lower, while overall sentiment remained stable, market sources said.

News
Noble sees tighter global drillship market, accelerating recovery in 2027
Contracted utilization hits record 99% mark
Day rates climb to $500,000 from $400,000
Exploration expected to drive 2027 demand
The global ultra-deepwater drilling market is tighter than current rig activity suggests, with contracted utilization at a record 99% and rising demand set to lift day rates and accelerate earnings growth through 2027, Noble Corp. management said Oct. 27.
Of the 110 ultra-deepwater rigs marketed worldwide, 109 are contracted for current or future work, above the 2024 peak of 105, CEO Robert Eifler said during the company's Q3 earnings call.
Only 88 rigs are currently working, Eifler said, about 10 fewer than at the 2024 peak, leaving a backlog of commitments yet to turn into active drilling.
"We've reached a point now where the number of pending contract startups for currently idle rigs modestly exceeds the number of contract rollovers over the next six months or so," Eifler said.
That could expand the active fleet in coming quarters, further tightening capacity and supporting day rates.
Recent high-end drillship contracts have set leading-edge day rates from the mid-$400,000s to $500,000/day, up from near $400,000/day earlier in the recovery, Eifler said, adding that rising utilization and a growing pipeline of future work were already influencing pricing, even before the active fleet nears full employment.
Contract awards have also reaccelerated in recent months after a slowdown between May and July, Noble said.
Exploration to drive next phase Higher oil prices have yet to lift offshore drilling demand,
Noble said. Producers remain disciplined and reluctant to make long-term
investment decisions on prices that could retreat as geopolitical conditions change, Eifler said.
Instead, the recovery has been driven mainly by previously delayed development projects now moving ahead.
Noble expects exploration, especially frontier drilling to replace reserves, to become a bigger demand driver as producers set 2027 budgets.
"We are very hopeful that as our customers exit budget season with really good cash flow during 2026, that we might actually see some more evidence of wildcat exploration in 2027," Eifler said.
Customers have increasingly discussed reserve replacement over the past three years, but that interest has yet to translate into a substantial increase in drilling contracts, Eifler said.
Even so, Noble sees a potential path to a fully contracted

© 2026 by S&P Global Inc. All rights reserved.

31

©2026 S&P Global Inc. Any unauthorized use of this copyrighted material constitutes infringement of our intellectual property and will subject you to possible legal action.

Platts Oilgram Price Report

October 27, 2026

high-end deepwater market within about a year, with additional 2028 demand likely to intensify competition for rigs.
Operators are not yet significantly accelerating projects over rig availability concerns, though urgency has increased over the past three months, Eifler cautioned.
"We're starting to hear more comments about how it's looking pretty tight going into 2028," he said.
Rising contracted utilization, firmer day rates and stronger forward coverage point to a recovery that could gather momentum even without a major price-driven jump in oil company spending.
Regional demand strengthens The recovery is broadening across offshore regions, though
demand remains uneven. In the US Gulf, Noble sees two or three opportunities nearing
firm commitments, which could lift the rig count from the midteens after it fell from the low 20s in recent years.
But Eifler warned that drillships leaving for work elsewhere could reduce available capacity and push Gulf day rates higher.
India is emerging as a potentially significant source of new demand, with Noble expecting requirements for about two to four high-end drillships starting in late 2027 and 2028.
West Africa and the Caribbean remain key markets, while new exploration opportunities are emerging along South America's Atlantic margin.
Noble recently secured drilling contracts offshore Nigeria, Ghana, Peru and Uruguay, plus a three-year well intervention deal in Angola. Its latest awards total about $560 million.

Houthi militants claim strike on Saudi Arabia's Ras Tanura refinery
Plant has not previously been attacked by Houthis
Missile attack ignites fires at facility: Houthis
NASA satellites show signs of fire at complex
Yemeni Houthi armed militias have claimed an attack on Saudi Arabia's 550,000 b/d Ras Tanura oil refinery, the first reported Houthi hit on the eastern plant since the militant group began targeting Saudi operations in late July, according to an Oct. 27 statement from a Houthi official.
"The Yemeni forces were able to target the Ras Tanura refinery in the Eastern Province with a ballistic missile, and the strike was precise," Houthi official Ameen Hayyan said on X, citing group spokesperson Yahya Saree.
The attack resulted in "massive fires breaking out at the targeted site," Hayyan added.
Saudi Aramco, which owns the plant, referred Platts to the Saudi Ministry of Energy for comment. The ministry said any statements from the kingdom would be issued through official government channels or the Saudi Press Agency. Saudi Arabia has not publicly commented on the reported attack or fire.

Security consultancy Vanguard Tech reported a fire near "Saudi Aramco facilities at Ras Tanura" early on Oct. 27, adding that this has subsequently been contained.
"The extent of damage and any impact on oil-loading operations remain unclear at current. The incident occurred as Saudi authorities reported intercepting three Houthi ballistic missiles targeting the kingdom's Eastern Region," Vanguard reported.
NASA's satellite-based fire detection map, FIRMS, showed signs of a fire at the Ras Tanura oil complex on Oct. 27, while Copernicus Sentinel-2 satellite imagery for Oct. 25 showed no damage at that time.
The Ras Tanura refinery was last targeted in early April, along with several other refineries, and, before that, was attacked twice in March, Platts has previously reported.
The refinery was temporarily shut after drone debris fell on its site March 2, following an attack at the beginning of the US-Israel war with Iran, the state-run Saudi Press Agency reported March 4.
On July 20, Houthi militias declared a "maritime embargo" and blockade of Saudi shipping operations. Since then, the militant group has intensified attacks on Saudi energy and civilian infrastructure.
The reported strike this week would represent the first known Houthi attack on Ras Tanura. Prior to this, Houthi militias have targeted the Riyadh refinery, the Jazan refinery, and unspecified Aramco facilities in Riyadh, the Eastern Region, Yanbu, Najran, Abha and Jazan, according to previous Houthi statements on X. The rebel group has also previously claimed to have attacked the 1 million b/d Shaybah oil field and the East-West pipeline.
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- Lulwa Albuainain, Lauren Holtmeier
ADNOC Drilling expects lower unconventional hydrocarbons activity after 2025 acceleration
Unconventional revenue falls to $67M in Q3
UAE targets 5 million b/d crude capacity by 2027
Fleet grows to 142 rigs in Abu Dhabi
ADNOC Drilling expects its unconventional hydrocarbon business to contribute reduced revenue for 2026 after a strongerthan-anticipated acceleration in 2025, the company said in an Oct. 27 statement.
The company's parent, state-run Abu Dhabi National Oil Co., is investing in unconventional oil and natural gas exploration and development as the UAE works to ramp up its crude production capacity to 5 million barrels/day by 2027 from 4.85 million b/d, according to the ADNOC website.
The unconventional business extracts hydrocarbons from tight reservoirs using well stimulation activities and is often compared to shale drilling in the US.
The company's unconventional business added $67 million to revenue in the third quarter, down from $158 million in Q3 2025 and $75 million in Q2 2026, ADNOC Drilling said in the statement.

Indonesia shifts crude, fuel, LPG procurement to government-led deals
Jakarta takes control of energy imports
New system bypasses Pertamina tenders
Lemigas to manage overseas purchases
Indonesia is shifting overseas procurement of crude oil, refined fuels and LPG away from Pertamina-led business-tobusiness tenders toward deals negotiated through governmentto-government and government-to-business channels, giving Jakarta a more direct role in securing the country's imported energy supplies.
The change is set out in Presidential Regulation No. 107/2026, signed by President Prabowo Subianto, and is intended to strengthen supply certainty amid geopolitical disruption, Coordinating Minister for Downstreaming and Energy Transition and Energy and Mineral Resources Minister Bahlil Lahadalia said Oct. 26.
"Until now, Pertamina has used a B2B process, and the tenders have taken a long time. Under this regulation, we are moving to G2G and G2B. This means we can now choose which countries to source supplies from," Lahadalia said at a press conference.
The move marks a significant change in Indonesia's procurement system, shifting a key part of the purchasing process from state-owned Pertamina to the government at a time of heightened concern over the security and reliability of imported energy supplies.
Under the new mechanism, the government will negotiate procurement arrangements with supplying countries, while a public service agency, or Badan Layanan Umum (BLU), will handle purchases and sell the crude, fuel, or LPG to Pertamina, Lahadalia said.
"There will be no other companies involved in the procurement chain. It will be country-to-country," he said.
Lahadalia said the existing B2B system had exposed Pertamina to supply uncertainty even after it secured tenders at competitive prices, with suppliers sometimes receiving higher

offers from other buyers after tenders had been awarded. The government plans to use Lemigas, the Energy and
Mineral Resources Ministry's oil and gas testing center, for the new import mechanism. Deputy Energy and Mineral Resources Minister Yuliot Tanjung said in May that the government planned to optimize existing institutions rather than establish a new BLU, including using Lemigas to import crude oil, refined fuels and LPG.
Supply security The overhaul comes amid uncertainty over energy supplies
linked to geopolitical tensions in the Middle East. The ministry's oil and gas director general said Oct. 5 that
fuel import tenders had attracted almost no participants amid the conflict, while securing alternative supplies could entail higher costs.
The new regulation retains a route for Pertamina to pursue direct imports when national reserves reach critically low levels or geopolitical disruptions affect a supplying country. Such purchases would have to be coordinated with the BLU, Lahadalia said.
Jakarta also plans to develop crude oil storage facilities in Indonesia, allowing imported barrels to be delivered directly into the country rather than transiting through third countries, according to Lahadalia.
"Our crude oil imports will also be supported by storage facilities that we plan to build here. The BLU will develop the facilities with several companies that have already partnered with it. This will allow imported crude to be shipped directly to Indonesia, without stopping in country A, country B or country C," he said.
The latest regulation builds on Presidential Regulation No. 26/2026, signed April 30, which allowed public service agencies and state-owned energy companies to import crude oil, refined fuels and LPG through arrangements including G2G agreements and transactions between the government and foreign suppliers.
The earlier regulation also allowed BLUs and state-owned energy companies to import during emergencies, including geopolitical or supply-chain disruptions and when national reserves fall below minimum thresholds.
Pertamina will coordinate with the Energy and Mineral Resources Ministry, BLU Lemigas, upstream regulator SKK Migas, and other relevant parties on implementation of the new procurement mechanism, the company's vice president for corporate communications, Muhammad Baron, said Oct. 26.
Pertamina will also ensure that its refineries, terminals, and distribution network are ready to support the new arrangements, he said.
Domestic crude and refinery output will remain the company's first priority, with Pertamina working with SKK Migas and production-sharing contractors to secure locally produced crude for domestic refineries, Baron said.
"Procurement from overseas complements domestic supplies to ensure that national demand is met," he said.
-- Mia Pei, Anita Nugraha

REFINERY NEWS: Citgo shut multiple units following Oct 21-22 third-party steam loss
Refinery: Corpus Christi, Texas
Owner: Citgo
Overall capacity: 165,000 b/d
Units affected: Multiple
Duration: Oct. 21-22
Citgo shut multiple process units at its Corpus Christi Refinery East Plant after a loss of steam from a third-party supplier triggered a sulfur dioxide emissions event, according to a filing with the Texas Commission on Environmental Quality on Oct. 27.
The Oct. 21-22 incident at the refinery's sulfur recovery unit (SRU) No. 1 incinerator is listed as an open, initial emissions event report and has been verified by agency staff, the TCEQ database shows.
TCEQ records indicate the upset began at 8:02 pm Oct. 21 and ended at 6:00 am Oct. 22. A company spokesperson was not available to comment on the current operational state of the refinery.
Citgo's Corpus Christi facility is supplied by a third-party cogeneration facility.

REFINERY NEWS: Valero isolates Corpus Christi BTX units after leak
Refinery: Corpus Christi, Texas
Owner: Valero
Overall capacity: 290,000 b/d
Units affected: BTX Unit
Duration: Oct. 23 4:45-5:00 am CT
Valero isolated equipment at the sulfolane BTX unit of its Corpus Christi, Texas, East refinery Oct. 23 after detecting a leak in a flare-line union, according to a filing made with local regulators.
In the filing made Oct. 26 with the Texas Commission on Environmental Quality, Valero estimated a release of 15 pounds of benzene during the 15-minute event.
The BTX unit pulls petrochemicals from reformate that can also be used in gasoline. If BTX production is reduced, more reformate could go into gasoline, potentially boosting supply and pressuring prices.
Platts assessed the US Gulf Coast premium CBOB 93 pipeline differential to NYMEX RBOB at plus 8.25 cents/gallon Oct. 23, down 0.75 cent/gal from Oct. 22, while the regular CBOB 87 differential moved to minus 15.75 cents/gal from minus 15 cents/ gal over the same period. The resulting premium-to-regular CBOB spread -- a key measure of high-octane blending economics -- held unchanged at 24 cents/gal on Oct. 22, Oct. 23 and Oct. 26. Platts is part of S&P Global Energy.

Refinery: Galveston Bay
Owner: Marathon Petroleum
Overall capacity: 631,000 b/d
Units affected: Coker, FCCU
Duration: Oct. 22 and Oct. 26
Marathon Petroleum reported two separate flaring-related emissions events at its Galveston Bay refinery in Texas City, Texas, to the Texas Commission on Environmental Quality (TCEQ) in late October.

REFINERY NEWS: India' BPCL to form subsidiary for Ramayapatnam refinery project
Refinery: Ramayapatnam refinery, Andhra Pradesh, India
Owner: Bharat Petroleum Corp. Ltd., or BPCL
Overall capacity: 10 million mt (310,000 b/d)
India's No. 2 state-run Bharat Petroleum Corp. Ltd. will set up a wholly owned subsidiary to execute a new refinery project at Ramayapatnam in the southern state of Andhra Pradesh, company officials said Oct. 27.
The greenfield refinery project would be developed as a 310,000 barrels/day refinery-cum-petrochemical project, they said.
The project will be developed with the required government, statutory and regulatory approvals, they said.
BPCL's board approved the formation of a dedicated subsidiary to execute the greenfield project, they said.
The company's board approved about $516 million for the new subsidiary to execute the new refinery project, they said.

A new detailed feasibility report (DFR) is in the final stages of completion, they said, and added that BPCL would make an investment decision on the project cost after its board approves the DFR.
The proposed refinery will have a 32% petrochemical intensity, they said.
Parallel activities to develop the greenfield refinery from the conceptual stage have already been initiated, including seeking environmental clearance from the federal government, they said.
BPCL is open to the idea of giving a 30% equity stake to other partners in the refinery project, they said.
The Andhra Pradesh government is expected to extend a 75% capital subsidy for 15-20 years for the new refinery project, they said, and added that the final framework was still being worked out.
The new refinery would help meet India's projected fueldemand growth of at least 7% over the next decades, they said.
BPCL operates three refineries at Mumbai, Kochi and Bina with a combined capacity of 706,000 b/d.

Subscriber Notes

Platts to amend rollover date of Asian Dated Brent Strip at London close
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, will amend the rollover date of its Asian Dated Brent (ADB) Strip at London close assessment to the first publication day of each month, effective Dec. 1, 2026.
The change follows a review of historical data sets and will align the ADB Strip at London close assessment with the current rollover date of the ADB Strip at Asia close assessment.
Currently, this assessment, which reflects the forward assessment of Dated Brent two months ahead of the date of publication at the 4:30 pm London close, rolls over on the 9th of the month.
Platts proposed this change in a subscriber note published Sept. 21, 2026.
The assessments affected by this change appear in Crude Oil Marketwire, Asia Pacific/Arab Gulf Marketscan, Oilgram Price Report, Platts Global Alert/ Platts Crude Oil Alert pages 2206, 2213, 2215, 2217, 2219, 2260, 2261, 2262, 2263, 2264, 2265, 2266, 2267, 2268, 2269, 2270, 2271, 2280, 2281, 2558, 2559, and under the following price database codes:

Platts to launch forward derivative assessments for STS Lome gasoline, diesel and jet fuel
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, has noted increasing demand for transparency on West African refined products, both on a spot market basis and for forward delivery. In response, Platts will start publishing daily forward swap assessments for the existing Gasoline STS Lome, Low Sulfur Diesel STS Lome, and Jet Fuel STS Lome physical price assessments, effective Nov. 30, 2026. These will be published as Month 1, Month 2, and Month 3 assessments.
In order to assess the derivatives on an editorial basis, Platts will consult a range of market information, including information heard in the physical market, relevant forward exchange-traded data, as well as related markets.
The published assessments will correspond with delivery dates three months forward, starting with the front month [M1], followed by M2 and M3.

Platts to launch STS Lome Gasoline differentials to Eurobob FOB AR assessments
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, will launch a new daily price assessment reflecting the differential between the Gasoline STS Lome assessment and the Gasoline Eurobob FOB AR barge assessment, effective Nov. 23, 2026.
This follows increased demand for transparency on West African gasoline differentials, as Nigeria's Dangote refinery continues to reshape flows and pricing dynamics in the Atlantic Basin.
The assessed differential is calculated as the difference between the physical Gasoline STS Lome assessment and the midpoint of the three-to-10-day forward strip, itself derived from the established Gasoline Eurobob FOB AR barge assessment derivative assessments.
The new assessments will provide a transparent indication of the value of STS Lome gasoline relative to Northwest European gasoline blendstock assessments, and will complement Platts existing outright STS Lome gasoline differentials against Platts Premium 10ppm FOB Mediterranean cargoes and Premium 10ppm FOB Rotterdam barges.

The new gasoline assessments will be found in European Marketscan and on Platts Global Alert and Platts Refined Products Alert fixed page 1122.

Platts to amend freight calculation for Oman, Murban CFR North Asia crude assessments Nov. 2
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, will amend the freight calculation used in its daily Oman and Murban CFR North Asia crude assessments, effective Nov. 2, 2026.
Under the change, Platts will use the daily VLCC Gulf of Omanto-Far East freight assessment in $/mt (DOOEW00), which was launched Aug. 3, in its calculation of the outright price of Oman and Murban delivered into North Asia at 4:30 pm Singapore time (0830 GMT).
The change will better reflect the shipping economics for the two grades, with Murban primarily loading from Fujairah port and Oman from Mina Al Fahal port outside the Strait of Hormuz.
Currently, the freight calculation is based on the daily VLCC Persian Gulf-to-Far East freight assessment in $/mt (TDAAB00).
Platts first proposed the change in a subscriber note published Sept. 28, 2026.
The freight calculation will continue to use the conversion factor for metric tons to barrels, as applied to the grades, in line with their prevailing qualities. The conversion factors are currently 7.32 for Oman and 7.62 for Murban.
In line with this change, Platts will also use the daily VLCC Gulf of Oman-to-Far East worldscale freight assessment (DIOEW00) in the freight calculation for Murban in its Indonesian Minas, Cinta and Widuri crude market parity prices.
Under the current MPP methodology for Minas, Cinta and Widuri, Murban -- as one of five basket grades -- is normalized for delivery to Singapore, accounting for freight. This is currently calculated using the daily VLCC Persian Gulf-to-Singapore worldscale freight assessment (PFAOD00).
The affected assessments appear in Crude Oil Marketwire, Oilgram Price Report, LNG Daily, Platts OPR World Crude Table, Platts Global Alert/Platts Crude Oil Alert pages 1250, 1805, 2202, 2203, 2211, 2216, 2217, 2244, 2245, 2264, 2265, 2280, 2281, 2811 and 2812, LNG Alert pages 0050 and 0051, Platts Natural Gas Alert pages 1024 and 1069, and under the price database codes below:

Platts amends Abu Dhabi crude OSPs publication Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, has updated its publication
of Abu Dhabi crude official selling prices effective Oct. 8, 2026, in line with changes announced by Abu Dhabi National Oil Co. (ADNOC).
In July, ADNOC said it would adopt a prompt-month pricing methodology based on the Platts Dubai benchmark (PCAAT00) plus an ADNOC-announced differential for the OSPs of its Abu Dhabi onshore and offshore crude grades, with effect from November 2026 loadings. ADNOC added that it would announce the OSP differentials for Murban, Das, Umm Lulu and Upper Zakum in the month prior to the delivery month.
Accordingly, Platts will publish the monthly OSP differentials for the four grades, basis Platts Dubai, beginning with the November differentials issued by ADNOC on Oct. 8, 2026.
Platts first announced the change in a subscriber note published on Aug. 31, 2026.
The OSP differentials are published under the following reinstated codes:

Grade Murban Umm Lulu Das Blend Upper Zakum

OSP Diff to Dubai AAIOS00 OSLUD00 AAXPL00 AAIOV00

Platts previously published the OSP differentials under these codes until May 2021, when they were then suspended due to a change in the ADNOC pricing methodology.
From November, the existing codes for the outright OSPs for Murban (AAIKZ00), Upper Zakum (AAILC00), Das Blend (AAXPH00) and Umm Lulu (OSLUF00) will remain unchanged. Platts will publish the outright OSPs of the four grades following the publication of the average of Platts Dubai crude assessments during the month of loading.
In view of the changes, Platts is no longer updating the codes reflecting Upper Zakum, Das Blend and Umm Lulu OSP differentials against Murban, as well as the codes for all four grades, including Murban, calculated against the monthly average of Platts cash Dubai 1st Line assessment in the month of trade.
The following codes will no longer be updated from October:

Grade Murban Umm Lulu Das Blend Upper Zakum

Platts to launch daily FOB Middle East bitumen assessments from Nov. 2
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, will launch daily VG30 and VG40 bitumen spot assessments for FOB Middle East, effective Nov. 2, 2026.
The new assessments will complement Platts' existing daily and weekly bitumen assessments, providing greater price transparency and information in the waterborne trade for the product in the Asia-Pacific and Middle East markets.
Platts will launch two new daily outright assessments and their respective monthly averages. These assessments will reflect bulk viscosity grade 30 and VG40 grade bitumen, as per IS 73:2013 specifications, loading 10-30 days forward from the date of publication in the Middle East.

Assessment FOB Middle East Bitumen VG30 FOB Middle East Bitumen VG40

Quantity 2,500-10,000 mt, bulk 2,500-10,000 mt, bulk

Laycan 10-30 days forward 10-30 days forward

UoM $/mt $/mt

Pricing information for material with other specifications, such as PEN 60/70, may be considered for the assessments, normalized to the proposed standard.
The assessments will reflect market value at the close of Asian trading at 4:30 pm Singapore time and follow the Singapore publication schedule. Bids, offers and trades reported throughout the day may be considered in the assessment process, with values normalized to the market close at 4:30 pm Singapore time.

Platts revises measurement units, descriptions for FO Licht ethanol prices
The following Platts FOLicht ethanol prices have had their units of measurement and descriptions updated, as summarized below.
Cubic Meter Pure Alcohol (CPA) · Type: Volume Unit · Definition: A unit of volume that measures the volume of pure (100%) alcohol. One CBMPA equals 1 cubic meter (1,000 liters) of pure alcohol. Unlike a standard cubic meter (m³), which measures the total volume of any substance (liquid, gas, or solid), CBMPA refers specifically to the ethanol content, not the total liquid volume.

Units of measurements and descriptions updated for Platts FOLicht ethanol prices The following Platts FOLicht ethanol prices have had their Units of measurements and descriptions updated, as summarized below. Hectoliter Pure Alcohol (HPA) · Type: Volume Unit · Definition: A unit of volume that measures the volume of pure (100%) alcohol. One HLTPA equals 1 hectoliter (100 liters) of pure alcohol. Unlike a standard hectoliter (hL), which measures the total volume of any liquid, HLTPA refers only to the pure alcohol content, regardless of the concentration in the original beverage.

Platts proposes to launch weighted indexes for Chicago CBOB, ULSD
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, is proposing to launch Chicago basket indexes for gasoline and diesel from March 2, incorporating the independent assessments for the major pipelines and Buckeye Complex and weighting them by trading volume, as well as adding a new, separate assessment for Chicago West Shore and Badger pipeline ULSD.
Feedback from market participants has indicated the need for Chicago-weighted basket indexes that reflect recent volumes traded on the Badger, West Shore, and Wolverine pipelines, as well as the Buckeye Complex.
The proposed weightings would be calculated by tracking recent trades across the West Shore, Badger, and Wolverine pipelines, as well as the Buckeye Complex. Total reported trades would be compiled on a cyclical basis, assuming a minimum volume of 10,000 barrels where not specified. These trades will then be sorted by pipeline to determine the percentage contribution of each component.
The weighted index would be calculated from the average of the differentials to NYMEX futures for the West Shore/Badger pipelines, Wolverine pipeline and BCX assessments, adjusted by their corresponding weightings.
For example, if Platts is assessing Cycle 2 for Chicago ULSD and traded volumes from the previous cycles came out to 20% West Shore and Badger, 30% Wolverine and 50% Buckeye Complex and the pipeline differentials were assessed at plus 2 cents/gal, plus 3 cents/gal and plus 3.50 cents/gal, respectively, the index basket differential would be plus 3.05 cents/gal.
Platts' current assessments for Chicago CBOB and ULSD are as follows:

Symbol AATHA00 AGCWC00 ACBAE00 AAREL00 ACBAA00 AGCWA00

Name ULSD Chicago pipeline ULSD Chicago Wolverine Pipeline ULSD Chicago BCX Gasoline CBOB Chicago pipeline Gasoline CBOB Chicago BCX Gasoline CBOB Chicago Wolverine Pipeline

The proposed Chicago basket indexes would be as follows:
Name Gasoline CBOB Chicago Basket ULSD Chicago Basket
Platts is concurrently seeking feedback on how to incorporate West Shore and Badger into the weighted index when the 1 psi waiver is in place during the summer. More information on how Platts will adjust the West Shore and Badger assessments in accordance with the 1 psi waiver can be found here.
Additionally, Platts seeks feedback on the naming conventions for the proposal. Platts proposes to amend the assessment name from Gasoline CBOB Chicago pipeline to Gasoline CBOB West Shore and Badger pipeline to better represent the products assessed. This change follows the Feb. 3, 2025, removal of the Wolverine Pipeline from the basket underpinning the generic Chicago assessment due to the anticipated removal of the 1 psi

Platts invites feedback on US gasoline RVP calendar for 2026 As part of its commitment to open and transparent pricing
and product specifications, Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, would like to invite feedback regarding publishing dates and/or cycles for 2026 US gasoline RVP shifts.
The 2026 calendar largely follows 2025 norms, with no notable deviations from prior years. The calendar will be updated throughout the year as more information becomes available.

The shift dates are based on relevant pipeline schedules, historical information, and feedback from market participants.
US Gulf Coast shifts are based on the Colonial Pipeline schedule. US Atlantic Coast shifts are based on Buckeye Pipeline and Colonial Pipeline calendars.
On the West Coast, Los Angeles, San Francisco and Arizona RVP changes are based on Kinder Morgan's SFPP schedule, while Portland and Seattle shifts are based on Olympic Pipeline's calendar.
In the Midwest, Group 3 shifts are based on the ONEOK Pipeline schedule, while Chicago shifts are based on Explorer Pipeline and Buckeye Complex schedules.
The US Environmental Protection Agency removed a waiver in eight Midwestern states that allowed for an additional 1 psi during the summer. Platts will adjust the RVP Calendar for Chicago CBOB in accordance with the implementation of the waiver. More information can be found here.
For questions or comments, please contact americas_products@spglobal.com with a cc to pricegroup@spglobal.com.
For written comments, please provide a clear indication if comments are not intended for publication by Platts for public viewing. Platts will consider all comments received and will make comments not marked as confidential available upon request.

3-DAY TRAINING

Oil Markets Fundamentals & Commercial Strategies

Nov 3-5, 2026 | Singapore

Dec 8-10, 2026 | Dubai

Mar 23-25, 2027 | Singapore Apr 6-8, 2027 | Houston

Apr 27-29, 2026 | London

May 11-12, 2027 | Abuja

Jun 22-24, 2027 | Istanbul

Jun 30-Jul 1 | Kuala Lumpur

Oil markets are increasingly complex, shaped by shifting supply and demand, geopolitical developments, changing trade flows and price volatility. This course provides a practical understanding of the forces shaping global crude oil and refined product markets.
Explore market fundamentals, price formation and forecasting, trading, and risk management, and learn how to translate market developments into better commercial decisions.
Build the market knowledge and commercial insight to make more confident decisions in a rapidly evolving oil market.
WHO SHOULD ATTEND The course would be suitable for:
· Technical and non-technical professionals working across crude oil and refined products
· Traders, brokers and market analysts · Professionals in commercial, planning, business development,
supply and distribution roles · Shipping and logistics professionals · New industry entrants seeking a practical understanding of oil
markets

2-DAY TRAINING
Upstream Fundamentals and Economics
June 2-3 | Houston

Fossil fuels dominate the energy mix representing over 80% of global energy demand. With geopolitical tensions influencing supply volumes and lower price, the S&P Global Commodity Insights "Upstream Fundamentals and Economics" course will equip you with the insights and strategies needed to navigate a constantly changing environment, meet challenges with informed decisions, and drive business growth.
Our prestigious Upstream Fundamentals and Economics Training is a great opportunity to hear from a range of S&P Global experts sharing their expertise, regional trends and industry insights, as well as concrete case studies and analyses.
WHO SHOULD ATTEND
This course would be suitable for:
· Technical and non-technical professionals, with experience or new recruits, from crude oil and refined products companies
